| [Assylum](https://www.assylum.com) | `assylum.com`, `assylum.com/?sessions`, `assylum.com/session//{id}` | Custom | No | Standalone `assylum`. 268 scenes. **The site publishes no complete listing and no sitemap** — `?sessions` shows twenty at a time and its `so=` parameter re-sorts rather than paginating, so sweeping `so=0…59` reaches 109 distinct sessions and plateaus there. What every session does have is a numeric `data-lid`, and `/session//{id}` serves it directly with no slug needed, so the walk is an **id sweep**: the highest id in reach is learned from the home page and six `?sessions&so=` views, and every id from 1 to that (plus a margin) is probed. A gap answers a near-empty 200 rather than a 404 and is not reported as a failure — most of the sweep lands on gaps. The sweep is by id, not date, so `KnownIDs` skips stored sessions rather than stopping. **Credits carry an in-fiction role label** (`Patient: Riley Reynolds`, `Nurse: …`) on 102 of 297 of them; keeping it filed the same person under two names, and stripping it collapsed 144 apparent performers to 127. Cast and date share one span, split on the date. |
| [AVIdolZ](https://avidolz.com) | `avidolz.com`, `avidolz.com/japan-porn/`, `avidolz.com/{slug}/` | WordPress (`vms_videos` post type) | No | ~369 scenes, frozen archive (nothing published since July 2020). WP REST is 401, so enumeration runs off `/wp-sitemap-posts-vms_videos-1.xml` via `wputil.RunWorkerPool`. **No page carries a publish date** — the sitemap `<lastmod>` is the only date source and is threaded into the parse (approximate: some entries share a bulk re-save timestamp). Cast and genres come from schema.org `itemprop="actor"`/`"genre"`/`"partOfSeries"` markup, which scopes them to the scene — bare `/jav-models/` and `/series/` links also appear in related-content blocks. Category pages share the root namespace with scenes, so the sitemap is the authoritative scene list. |
| [Amateur Allure](https://www.amateurallure.com) | `amateurallure.com`, `amateurallure.com/tour/scenes/{slug}_vids.html` | NATS tour | No | `/tour/updates/page_{N}.html` listing (~919) + concurrent detail fetch (og title/description, performers); date from listing |
| [Amateur Allure Classics](https://www.amateurallureclassics.com) | `amateurallureclassics.com/`, `amateurallureclassics.com/categories/movies.html`, `amateurallureclassics.com/scenes/{slug}_vids.html` | ElevatedX CMS | No | Amateur Allure child; ElevatedX Classic template served from the document root via `julesjordanutil` (BasePath `/`) — `update_details` cards, `/categories/movies_{N}_d.html` listing, detail worker pool |
| [Big Booty TGirls](https://bigbootytgirls.com) | `bigbootytgirls.com`, `bigbootytgirls.com/categories/updates_{N}_d.html` | Elevated X (`updateItem` tour at the document root) | No | Thin wrapper around `darkreachupdateitemutil` — that util is named for the network it was written against, but the template is a vendor one and already covers hammerboys.tv and terrorxxx. The one Trans500 child with a live catalogue of its own: ~312 scenes over 26 pages of 12, listing-only (title, date, performers, thumbnail; the tour exposes no duration, tags or pricing). **Model pages are not a supported mode here** — this site renders them as an `update_table_right` block rather than `updateItem` cards, so a `/models/` URL reports a parse error instead of quietly returning nothing. The full listing reaches every scene anyway. |
| Erika Lust Films (3 sites) | `erikalust.com`, `erikalust.com/film/{slug}`, `lustcinema.com`, `lustcinema.com/movies/{slug}`, `xconfessions.com`, `xconfessions.com/film/{slug}` | Nuxt 2 (SSR) | No | Table-driven `erikalust` package: Erika Lust Films (574), Lust Cinema (528), XConfessions (334). Enumeration runs off `/sitemap.xml`, which is **gzip served with no `.gz` on the URL and no `Content-Encoding`** — the transport does not unwrap it, so every sitemap body is gunzipped explicitly or it reads as binary. The sitemaps mix scenes with performers, directors, confessions and per-scene `/watch/trailer|bhs|chapter/{id}` sub-pages, so only a single-segment `/{prefix}/{slug}` counts (19 of XConfessions' 353 entries are sub-pages). Lust Cinema uses `/movies/`, the other two `/film/`. **Two scene templates, read field-by-field rather than branched on**: the film template has an `h1` and linked cast, the movie template has no `h1` at all (title comes from `<title>` minus the storefront's own name, which the site spells without a space) and links its cast by image with the name in `title=`. **Date and runtime live only in the Nuxt payload** — the page shows a bare year and a rounded "12 min". The payload is minified JS with variable indirection (`t.release_date=N`), so it is not parsed: the one `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` literal is lifted by shape and kept only when its year matches the rendered year. **The poster also comes from the payload** (`poster_picture`) — the first `<img>` on every template is the site logo, so an img fallback stores an SVG logo as the thumbnail. Erika Lust lists duo entities ("Jet Setting Jasmine & King Noire") next to both members; a composite credit is dropped only when every one of its `&`-separated parts is separately credited on the same scene, so a stage name containing `&` survives. Lust Cinema publishes no per-scene categories. |

Duration is unusable — the only runtimes on the page belong to preview clips. The last three sites are absent from the CMS's own embedded site list and were found via the StashDB tree. |

Each page also renders a future-dated "coming soon" sidebar reusing the `update_date` class, so every field is scoped to an `update_block`. Pages past the end return HTTP 200 with an empty template, so end-of-listing is zero cards. |

The API exposes no sort parameter and returns items roughly oldest-first, so the `KnownIDs` early-stop is dropped — it would halt on the first stored scene and never reach anything newer. **The WAF challenges on request count, and answers with JavaScript under HTTP 200** — a `leastFactor` page in place of the JSON, from roughly the seventeenth request in a window on. It is not about spacing: 25 back-to-back requests run clean, while a slow walk is challenged at 18. That is why this scraper worked for years and then began stopping mid-catalogue — a profile passed ~340 scenes, which at 20 per page is more than seventeen requests. **The fix is to ask for fewer pages, not to go slower**: the API caps `pageSize` at 300 by its own error message (`"The page size must not be greater than 300."`) and the scraper sends exactly that, turning a 1,096-scene profile into four requests. Also here: `httpx.XHRHeaders` rather than `BrowserHeaders` (navigation headers on a JSON POST are challenged sooner), and `httpx.DoJSON` so a challenge that still lands is retried and reported for what it is rather than as `invalid character '<'`. If a client does get challenged anyway, the remaining discriminator is below the header layer — with identical headers curl walks clean while Go is challenged on every request, so it is Go's HTTP/2 fingerprint, and `NewBrowserTLSClient` does not help (a Chrome hello over Go's h2 is still Go's h2). Emulating a browser's h2 frames or running the challenge JS would be defeating bot detection, which FSS does not do; the supported way through is `--site-cookie mydirtyhobby="KEY=…"`, copied from a browser that passed the gate. |

The HTTP timeout is 60s, deliberately above the API's ~30s gateway ceiling, so late-but-valid responses are not cancelled client-side. `RecommendedDelay` = 300ms, warned via `WarnDelayBelow`. Raising `--workers` past a modest value does not speed up a full scrape — it only widens the detail pool, which is never the bottleneck, and the page pool is capped independently. Every link on either page goes to `/signup` or `/login`. |

## Adding a new scraper

See the [contributing guide](../CONTRIBUTING.md) for step-by-step instructions and reference implementations.
| [XXX Job Interviews](https://xxxjobinterviews.com) | `xxxjobinterviews.com`, `xxxjobinterviews.com/videos/` | MechBunny | No | Standalone `xxxjobinterviews`. Listing at `/videos/` then `/videos/page/{N}`, six cards a page over ~39 pages; the card gives title, runtime, date and thumbnail and `/video/{slug}-{id}.html` adds the cast (`/pornstars/` links), a trailer mp4 and — **only in its meta tags** — the description and keyword list, from which the cast names are filtered back out. The date carries an English ordinal ("August 22nd, 2026"), stripped before parsing, and the CDN URLs are protocol-relative. Details are fetched a page at a time so scenes stream out rather than arriving in one burst. |
